Subject: Notice convening the 19th Annual General Meeting and Annual Report for the Financial  
              Year 2025-26 
 
 
Dear Sir/Madam, 
 
Pursuant to Regulation 30, 34 and any other applicable Regulation of the SEBI (Listing Obligations and 
Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 (“ SEBI Listing Regulations”), please find enclosed the 
Notice of Nineteenth Annual General Meeting (“19th AGM”) and the Annual Report of Yatharth 
Hospital & Trauma Care Services Limited (“Company”) for the financial year 2025-26, which is being 
sent through electronic mode to the Members whose email address is registered with the 
Company/Registrar & Share Transfer Agent (‘RTA’)/Depository Participant(s) (‘DPs’). 
 
Further, in terms of Regulation 36(1)(b) of the SEBI Listing Regulations, a letter containing the web-
link, exact path and QR Code comprising the complete details of Notice convening 19th AGM and 
Annual Report for the financial year 2025-26, is sent to those members who have not registered their 
e-mail address with the Company/ RTA/ DP. A copy of letter that is dispatched to members is 
enclosed as Annexure-I.  
  
The 19th AGM of the Members of the Company is scheduled to be held on Friday, September 25, 2026 
at 12:00 Noon (IST) through Video Conferencing (“VC”) / Other Audio-Visual Means (“OAVM”) in 
accordance with the applicable circulars issued by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs and Securities and 
Exchange Board of India.  
 
The Company has provided the e-voting facility to its Members to exercise their right to vote on the 
resolutions proposed to be passed at the 19th AGM. The cut-off date i.e. Friday, September 18, 2026 
for the purpose of determining the Members eligible to vote on the resolutions set out in the Notice. 
 
Pursuant to Regulation 44 of SEBI Listing Regulations, Company is providing facility for remote e-
voting to its members as per the below schedule of events relating to AGM: - 
 
 
 
 
 
Events Day & Date  Time 
Cut-off Date,  to determine the 
Members eligible to vote on the 
resolutions set out in the 
Notice. Friday, September 18, 2026 NA

E-Voting End  Thursday, September 24, 2026  5:00 P.M (IST)

The aforesaid AGM Notice and Annual Report will also be available on the website of the company at 
https://www.yatharthhospitals.com/investors   
 
This is for your kind information and records. 
 
Thanking You 
 
Yours Faithfully, 
For Yatharth Hospital & Trauma Care Services Limited

NOTICE OF 19TH ANNUAL GENERAL MEETING
NOTICE is hereby given that the 19th (Nineteenth) 
Annual General Meeting (“AGM ”) of the Members of 
Yatharth Hospital & Trauma Care Services Limited 
(“Company”) will be held on Friday, September 25, 
2026 at 12:00 Noon (IST) through Video Conferencing/
Other Audio Visual Means (“VC/OAVM”) to transact 
the following businesses:
ORDINARY BUSINESS:
1. To consider and adopt: (a) the audited standalone 
financial statements of the Company for the 
financial year ended March 31, 2026 and the 
reports of the Board of Directors and the Auditors 
thereon; and (b) the audited consolidated financial 
statements of the Company for the financial year 
ended March 31, 2026 and the report of Auditors 
thereon and in this regard to consider and if 
thought fit, to pass the following resolutions as 
Ordinary Resolutions:
 (i) “RESOLVED THAT the audited standalone 
financial statements of the Company for the 
financial year ended March 31, 2026 and the 
reports of the Board of Directors and Auditors 
thereon as circulated to the members, be and 
are hereby considered and adopted.” 
 (ii) “RESOLVED THAT the audited consolidated 
financial statements of the Company for 
the financial year ended March 31, 2026 
and the report of Auditors thereon as 
circulated to the members, be and are hereby 
considered and adopted.”
2. To appoint Dr. Ajay Kumar Tyagi (DIN: 01792886) 
who retires by rotation as Director and, being 
eligible, offers himself for re-appointment:
 To consider and if thought fit, to pass the following 
resolution as an Ordinary Resolution: 
 “RESOLVED THAT pursuant to Section 152 and 
other applicable provisions, if any, of the Companies 
Act, 2013 and the Rules made thereunder, Dr. 
Ajay Kumar Tyagi (DIN: 01792886), who retires by 
rotation at this Annual General Meeting and being eligible offers himself for re-appointment, be and is 
hereby re-appointed as a Director of the Company, 
liable to retire by rotation.”

(or such other adjusted figure for any bonus, stock

exercisable into not more than 2,50,000 (Two Lakh 
Fifty Thousand) Equity Shares (“Shares”) of face

for any bonus, stock splits or consolidations or 
other reorganization of the capital structure of the 
Company as may be applicable from time to time 
as defined in the scheme) on such terms and in 
such manner as the Board of Directors may decide 
in accordance with the provisions of the applicable 
laws and the provisions of the Scheme.
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT the Scheme 
shall be administered by the Nomination and 
Remuneration Committee of the Company who 
shall have all necessary powers as defined in the 
Scheme and is hereby designated as Compensation 
Committee in pursuance of the SEBI (SBEB & SE) 
Regulations, 2021. 
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT the Scheme shall be 
implemented through Direct route, for extending 
the benefits to the eligible Employees by the way 
of fresh allotment from the Company.
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT the Nomination 
and Remuneration Committee be and is hereby 
authorized to issue and allot Equity Shares upon 
exercise of Options from time to time in accordance 
with the Scheme and such Equity Shares shall rank 
pari passu in all respects with the then existing 
Equity Shares of the Company.
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT the Company shall 
conform to the applicable Accounting Policies, 
Guidelines or Accounting Standards as may 
be applicable from time to time, including the 
disclosure requirements prescribed therein.
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT in case of any corporate 
action(s) such as rights issues, bonus issues, stock 
splits, consolidation of shares, the outstanding 
options to be granted under the Scheme shall be 
suitably adjusted for the number of options as 
well as the exercise price in a fair and reasonable 
manner, in accordance with the Scheme.
 RESOLVED FURTHER THAT the Nomination and 
Remuneration Committee of the Company is 
authorized to devise, formulate, evolve, decide 
upon and bring into effect the Scheme as per 
the terms approved in this resolution read with the conditions, if any, with which consent from 
Members is received and at any time to modify, alter 
or amend the said terms subject to compliance 
with the SEBI (SBEB & SE) Regulations, 2021 and 
other applicable laws, rules and regulations, as may 
be prevailing at that time.

Act’), SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure

(‘the Listing Regulations’), MCA Circulars and SEBI 
Circulars, the AGM of the Company is being held 
through VC/ OAVM which does not require physical 
presence of members at a common venue. The 
proceedings of the AGM will be deemed to be 
conducted at the Registered Office of the Company 
which shall be the deemed Venue of the AGM.
3. In terms of the MCA & SEBI Circulars since the 
AGM is being held through VC / OAVM, the 
requirement of physical attendance of Members 
has been dispensed with, there is no requirement of 
appointment of proxies. Accordingly, the facility of

Member whose name appears as the first holder in 
the order of names as per the Register of Members 
of the Company will be entitled to vote.

 THE INTRUCTIONS OF SHAREHOLDERS FOR 
E-VOTING AND JOINING VIRTUAL MEETINGS 
ARE AS UNDER:
 Step 1 : Access through Depositories CDSL/NSDL 
e-Voting system in case of individual    shareholders 
holding shares in demat mode. 
 Step 2 : Access through CDSL e-Voting system 
in case of shareholders holding shares in 
physical mode and non-individual shareholders 
in demat mode. 
 (i) The voting period begins on September 22, 
2026 from 09:00 AM and ends on September 
YATHARTH SUPER SPECIALITY HOSPITAL10
24, 2026 till 05:00 PM. During this period 
shareholders’ of the Company, holding shares 
either in physical form or in dematerialized 
form, as on the cut-off date (record date) 
of September 18, 2026 may cast their vote 
electronically. The e-voting module shall be 
disabled by CDSL for voting thereafter.
 (ii) Shareholders who have already voted prior 
to the meeting date would not be entitled to 
vote at the meeting day.
 (iii) Pursuant to SEBI Circular No. SEBI/HO/CFD/
CMD/CIR/P/2020/242 dated December 9, 
2020 under Regulation 44 of Securities and 
Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 
2015, listed entities are required to provide 
remote e-voting facility to its shareholders, 
in respect of all shareholders’ resolutions. 
However, it has been observed that the 
participation by the public non-institutional 
shareholders/retail shareholders is at a 
negligible level. 
  Currently, there are multiple e-voting service 
providers (ESPs) providing e-voting facility 
to listed entities in India. This necessitates 
registration on various ESPs and maintenance 
of multiple user IDs and passwords by 
the shareholders.   In order to increase the efficiency of the voting 
process, pursuant to a public consultation, 
it has been decided to enable e-voting to 
all the demat account holders, by way of a 
single login credential, through their demat 
accounts/ websites of Depositories/ Depository 
Participants. Demat account holders would 
be able to cast their vote without having to 
register again with the ESPs, thereby, not 
only facilitating seamless authentication 
but also enhancing ease and convenience of 
participating in e-voting process. 
  Step 1: Access through Depositories CDSL/
NSDL e-Voting system in case of individual    
shareholders holding shares in demat mode. 
 (iv) In terms of SEBI circular no. SEBI/HO/CFD/
CMD/CIR/P/2020/242 dated December 9, 
2020  on e-Voting facility provided by Listed 
Companies, Individual shareholders holding 
securities in demat mode are allowed to vote 
through their demat account maintained 
with Depositories and Depository Participants. 
Shareholders are advised to update their 
mobile number and email Id in their demat 
accounts in order to access e-Voting facility.

method for e-Voting and joining virtual

Individual

Depository1) Users who have opted for CDSL Easi / Easiest facility, can login through their 
existing user id and password. Option will be made available to reach e-Voting 
page without any further authentication. The users to login to Easi / Easiest 
are requested to visit cdsl website www.cdslindia.com and click on login icon & 
New System Myeasi Tab.
2) After successful login the Easi / Easiest user will be able to see the e-Voting option 
for eligible companies where the e-Voting is in progress as per the information 
provided by company. On clicking the evoting option, the user will be able to see 
e-Voting page of the e-Voting service provider for casting your vote during the 
remote e-Voting period or joining virtual meeting & voting during the meeting. 
Additionally, there is also links provided to access the system of all e-Voting Service 
Providers, so that the user can visit the e-Voting service providers’ website directly.
3) If the user is not registered for Easi/Easiest, option to register is available at cdsl 
website www.cdslindia.com and click on login & New System Myeasi Tab and then 
click on registration option.
4) Alternatively, the user can directly access e-Voting page by providing Demat 
Account Number and PAN No. from a e-Voting link available on   www.cdslindia.
com  home page. The system will authenticate the user by sending OTP on 
registered Mobile & Email as recorded in the Demat Account. After successful 
authentication, user will be able to see the e-Voting option where the evoting 
is in progress and also able to directly access the system of all e-Voting Service 
Providers.

 1.  Mr. Saurav Upadhyay, a Practicing Company 
Secretary (Certificate of Practice No. 25283, 
Membership No. ACS 67860), Proprietor, M/s. USRK & Company has been appointed 
as the Scrutinizer to scrutinize the e-voting 
and remote e-voting process in a fair and 
transparent manner. 
 2.  The Scrutinizer shall after the conclusion of 
voting at the AGM, will first count the votes 
cast at the meeting and thereafter unblock 
the votes cast through remote e-Voting in the 
presence of at least two witnesses not in the 
employment of the Company and shall make, 
not later than 2 working days of the conclusion 
of the AGM, a consolidated scrutinizer’s report 
of the total votes cast in favour or against, if 
any, to the Chairman or a person authorized by 
him in writing, who shall countersign the same 
and declare the result of the voting forthwith. 
 3.  The results shall be declared not later than 2 
working days of the conclusion of the AGM 
by posting the same on the website of the 
Company www.yatharthhospitals.com and 
shall simultaneously forward the results to 
BSE Limited and National Stock Exchange 
of India Ltd., where the securities of the 
Company are listed. 
 4.  Subject to receipt of requisite number of 
votes, the Resolution shall be deemed to 
be passed on the date of the Meeting i.e. 
September 25, 2026.

Item No. 3
The Company is required to have its costs records 
audited by a Cost Accountant in practice. Accordingly, 
the Board of Directors of the Company on the 
recommendation of the Audit Committee, have 
approved the appointment of M/s. Subodh Kumar & 
Co., Cost Accountant (Registration No. 104250) as Cost 
Auditors of the Company for conducting the audit 
of the cost records of the Company, for the financial 
year 2026-27 at a remuneration of H 75000/- (Rupees 
Seventy-five Thousand only), exclusive of out-of-pocket 
expenses and applicable taxes, payable to Cost Auditors 
in connection with the aforesaid audit. 
Pursuant to Section 148 of the Companies Act, 2013 read 
with the Companies (Audit and Auditors) Rules, 2014, 
members of the Company are required to ratify the 
remuneration proposed to be paid to the Cost Auditors 
and pass the resolution as an ordinary resolution. 
Accordingly, the Board of Directors recommends 
the ordinary resolution set out at item no. 3 of the 
accompanying notice for approval by the members. 
None of the Directors, Key Management Personnel 
of the Company and their relatives, are in any way 
concerned or interested financially or otherwise at item 
no. 3 of the notice. 
The Board recommends the ordinary resolution 
as set out in item no. 3 of the notice for approval 
by the members.
Item No. 4
The Company proposes to enter into a Related Party 
Transaction with M/s. MGS Infotech Research and 
Solutions Private Limited, Subsidiary Company, a 
related party of the Company, for giving loan amounting 
to  J 150 Crore in one or more tranches for the purpose 
of principal business activities.Pursuant to Rule 15(3) of the Companies (Meetings of 
Board and its Powers) Rules, 2014, prior approval of 
the members of the Company by way of an Ordinary 
Resolution is required where the transaction or 
transactions exceed the prescribed threshold.
Further, pursuant to Regulation 23 of the SEBI 
(Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015, a Related Party Transaction which 
qualifies as a Material Related Party Transaction is 
required to be approved by the shareholders of the 
Company. A Related Party Transaction is considered 
material where the transaction(s), individually or 
taken together with previous transaction(s) during a 
financial year, exceed 10% of the annual consolidated 
turnover of the listed entity as per its last audited 
financial statements.
The proposed Related Party Transaction was placed 
before the Audit Committee of the Company, which, 
after considering the terms, conditions, commercial 
rationale and other relevant information relating to 
the proposed transaction as approved by the audit 
committee and recommended by the Board to the 
members. The Board of Directors, thereafter, considered 
proposed transaction for the approval of the members 
of the Company.
The proposed transaction is to be undertaken on an 
arm’s length basis and for the principal business activity 
of the Company, and the Board of Directors is of the 
opinion that the proposed transaction is in the interest 
of the Company.
The Audit Committee has reviewed the certificate(s) 
provided by the CEO/Managing Director/Whole-time 
Director/Manager and the Chief Financial Officer of 
the Company in relation to the proposed Related Party 
Transaction(s), in accordance with the applicable RPT 
Industry Standards.
In accordance with the applicable SEBI Industry 
Standards on “Minimum information to be provided 
to the  Shareholders for approval of Related Party 
Transactions”, the following information is provided 
to the members:

Item No. 5
The Company had undertaken an Initial Public Offering (“IPO”) in the Financial Year 2023-24. Pre-IPO Placement 
by way of Private Placement of 4,000,000 equity shares for cash at a price of ₹ 300 per equity share aggregating to  
₹ 1,200.00 Million. The total fund raised from pre IPO and IPO  was ₹ 6,100.00 Million. 
After adjusting the expenses incurred in connection with the IPO, the net proceeds were proposed to be utilised 
towards the objects of the Issue as specified in the Prospectus of the Company. The utilisation of the IPO proceeds 
is being undertaken in accordance with the objects stated in the Prospectus and the details of utilisation thereof 
are set out below:

3. The reason and justification for seeking 
revised proposal in the objects of the IPO
 The Company and its subsidiaries (subsidiaries 
means: AKS Medical & Research Centre Private 
Limited, Ramraja Multispeciality Hospital & Trauma 
Centre Private Limited, Pristine Infracon Private 
Limited, MGS Infotech Research and Solutions 
Private Limited and Shanti Ved Institute of Medical 
Sciences Private Limited) as operating in the 
healthcare sector, where timely investment in 
hospitals and healthcare infrastructure is critical 
to support expansion, enhance medical facilities 
and improve patient care. Since the completion of 
the Initial Public Offering ("IPO"), the Company's 
business has continued to expand, resulting in 
evolving funding requirements across the group.
 The IPO proceeds were originally proposed to 
be utilised as per the original expected timeline 
towards, inter alia, repayment, in full or part, 
of borrowings availed by the subsidiaries, AKS 
Medical & Research Centre Private Limited ("AKS") 
and Ramraja Multispeciality Hospital & Trauma 
Centre Private Limited ("Ramraja"), and for funding 
capital expenditure and expenses of the respective 
hospitals operated by them. However, as reflected 
in the Monitoring Agency Report for the quarter 
ended June 30, 2026, a portion of the IPO proceeds 
allocated for the aforesaid objects remained 
unutilised as of June 30, 2026. While the original 
objects of the Issue continue to remain relevant, 
the Board believes that restricting the utilisation 
of the remaining proceeds solely to AKS Medical 
& Research Centre Private Limited and Ramraja 
Multispeciality Hospital & Trauma Centre Private 
Limited may constrain the Company's ability to 
deploy capital efficiently in line with the current 
and future funding requirements of its expanding 
subsidiary network.
 Accordingly, in order to facilitate the growth and 
expansion of the Company's healthcare business, 
optimise capital allocation and ensure efficient utilisation of the unutilised IPO proceeds, the 
Board has proposed to broaden the scope of the 
relevant objects of the Issue to permit utilisation of 
the remaining IPO proceeds towards repayment/
prepayment of borrowings and funding of 
capital expenditure in the Company and/or its 
subsidiaries, based on their respective business 
and funding requirements.
 The proposed utilisation does not alter the 
fundamental purpose or nature of the IPO 
proceeds. The funds will continue to be utilised 
for the same broad objects, namely repayment/
prepayment of borrowings and funding of capital 
expenditure in the Company and/or subsidiaries. 
The proposed utilisation merely provides the 
Company with the operational flexibility to deploy 
the unutilised IPO proceeds.
 Pursuant to Section 27 of the Companies Act, 2013 
read with Rule 7 of the Companies (Prospectus 
and Allotment of Securities) Rules, 2014 and the 
applicable provisions of the SEBI (Issue of Capital 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2018 
and the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure 
Requirements) Regulations, 2015, the proposed 
utilisation requires the approval of the Members by 
way of a Special Resolution. All relevant documents, 
including the copy of the Notice, the proposed 
resolution and this Explanatory Statement relating 
to the proposed utilization of funds as defined 
in clause no. 3.
 Members are requested to refer to Note 
No. 16 of the Notice for details regarding 
inspection of documents.
 None of the Directors or Key Managerial Personnel 
of the Company or their relatives is, in any way, 
concerned or interested, financially or otherwise, in 
the proposed Special Resolution.
 Accordingly, the Board of Directors recommends 
the Special Resolution set out at Item No. 5 of the 
Notice of this AGM for approval by the Members
NOTICE21
Item No. 6 and 7
Equity based remuneration includes alignment of 
personal goals of the Employees with Organisational 
objectives by participating in the ownership of the 
Company. The Board of Directors of your Company 
understands the need to enhance the employee 
engagement, to reward the employees for their 
association and performance as well as to motivate 
them to contribute to the growth and profitability 
of the Company. 
In order to reward and retain the employees and to 
create a sense of ownership and participation amongst 
them, the Board of Directors (hereinafter referred to as 
the “Board of Directors” which term shall be deemed 
to include any Committee, including the Nomination 
and Remuneration Committee) has in its meeting 
held on August 10, 2026, approved Yatharth Hospital 
& Trauma Care Services Employee Stock Option 
Scheme – 2026 (“Scheme”) to or for the benefit of such 
Employee as defined in the Scheme and explained in 
the explanatory statement.
In terms of Regulation 6(1) of Securities and Exchange 
Board of India (Share Based Employee Benefits and 
Sweat Equity) Regulations, 2021 (“SEBI (SBEB & SE) 
Regulations”) and Section 62 and other applicable 
provisions of the Companies Act, 2013, issue of Shares 
under an Employee Stock Options Scheme requires 
an approval of the existing Members by way of Special 
Resolution. The Special Resolutions set out at Item No. 6 
is seeking your approval for the said purpose.
Further, as per Regulation 6(3)(c) of SEBI (SBEB & SE) 
Regulations, approval of the shareholders by way of 
separate Special Resolution is also required for grant 
of Options to Employees of a Subsidiary Companies of 
the Company in India. The Special Resolution set out at 
Item No.7 is to seek your approval for the said purpose.
The salient features and other details of the Scheme as 
required pursuant to Regulation 6(2) of SEBI (SBEB &SE) 
Regulations are as under:
a) Brief Description of the Scheme: 
 The Scheme shall be called Yatharth Hospital & 
Trauma Care Services Employee Stock Option 
Scheme – 2026 (“Scheme”).
 The Purpose of the Scheme includes the following: 
 i. To attract relevant talent into the Company to 
drive its growth plans.
 ii. To motivate the Employees to contribute to 
the growth and profitability of the Company.
 iii. To retain the Employees and reduce the 
attrition rate of the Company. iv. To achieve sustained growth and the creation 
of shareholder value by aligning the interests 
of the Employees with the long-term interests 
of the Company. 
 v. To foster a sense of ownership and participation 
amongst the Employees, allowing them to 
share in the value they create for the Company 
in the years to come, and
 vi. To provide additional deferred 
rewards to Employees.
b) The total number of Options to be offered and 
granted under the Scheme:
 The maximum number of Options that may be 
granted in one or more tranches, pursuant to this 
Scheme shall not exceed 2,50,000 (Two Lakh Fifty 
Thousand) Options which shall be convertible into 
equal number of Shares not exceeding 2,50,000 
(Two Lakh Fifty Thousand) Equity Shares having 
face value of H 10/- each.
 If any Option granted under the Scheme lapses 
or is forfeited or surrendered under any provision 
of the Scheme, such Option shall be available for 
further grant under the Scheme unless otherwise 
determined by the Committee.
 Further, the maximum number of Options that 
can be granted and the Shares arise upon exercise 
of these Options shall stand adjusted in case of 
Corporate Action (as defined in the Scheme).
c) Identification of classes of Employees entitled 
to participate in the Scheme:
 (a) An Employee as designated by the Company, 
who is exclusively working in India or 
outside India; or
 (b) A Director of the Company, whether a Whole 
Time Director or not, including a non-executive 
Director who is not a Promoter or member 
of the Promoter Group, but excluding an 
Independent Director; or
 (c) An employee as defined in (a) or (b), of a 
Subsidiary Company, in India.
 but does not include
 (a) An Employee who is a Promoter or a person 
belonging to the Promoter Group; or
 (b) A Director who either himself or through 
his Relative or through any Body Corporate 
directly or indirectly, holds more than ten 
percent of the outstanding equity shares 
of the Company.
YATHARTH SUPER SPECIALITY HOSPITAL22
d) Requirement of Vesting and period of Vesting:
 Vesting Period shall commence from a period of 1 
(One) year from the Grant Date and shall extend 
upto a maximum period of 4 (Four) years from 
the Grant Date, at the discretion of and in the 
manner prescribed by the Committee and set out 
in the Grant Letter.
 The actual vesting would be subject to the 
continued employment of the Grantee and may 
further be linked with certain performance and 
other criteria, as determined by the Committee 
and mentioned in the Grant Letter.
e) Maximum period within which the Options 
shall be vested:
 Maximum period within which the Options shall be 
vested is 4 (Four) years from the Date of Grant. 
f) Exercise Price or Pricing Formula:
 Under this Scheme, the Exercise Price will 
be decided by the Committee at the time of 
grant and shall be linked with Market Price as 
defined in the Scheme.
 The Committee has the power to provide a suitable 
discount on such price as arrived above. However, 
in any case the Exercise Price shall not go below 
the face value of Share of the Company.
g) Exercise period and process of Exercise:
 After Vesting, Options can be Exercised either 
wholly or partly, within a maximum exercise period 
of 2 (Two) years from the date of respective vesting, 
after submitting the Exercise application along 
with payment of the Exercise Price, applicable 
taxes and other charges, if any. The Committee 
may open a quarterly Exercise window, during the 
overall exercise period, as per their own discretion.
 The mode and manner of the exercise shall be 
communicated to the Grantees individually.
h) Appraisal process for determining the 
eligibility of the Employees to the scheme:
 The Committee may on the basis of all or any of 
the following criteria, decide on the Employees 
who are eligible for the Grant of Options under the 
Scheme, the number of Options to be Granted and 
the terms and conditions thereof.
	 Longevity of Service: It will be determined on the 
basis of tenure of employment of an Employee 
in the Company/ Subsidiary Company.
	 Performance of Employee: Employee’s 
performance during the financial year in the 
Company/ Subsidiary Company on the basis of 
decided parameters.	 Performance of Company: Performance of 
the Company/ Subsidiary Company as per the 
standards to be set by the Committee/ Board 
of Directors from time to time.
	 Any other criteria as decided by the Committee 
in consultation with Board of Directors 
from time to time.
i) The Maximum number of Options to be 
granted per Employee and in aggregate:
 Subject to availability of Options in the pool under 
the Scheme, the maximum number of Options that 
can be granted to any eligible Employee during 
any one year shall not be equal to or exceed 1% of 
the issued capital (excluding outstanding warrants 
and conversions) of the Company at the time of 
Grant. The Committee may decide to Grant such 
number of Options equal to or exceeding 1% of the 
issued capital (excluding outstanding warrants 
and conversions) to any eligible Employee as the 
case may be, subject to the separate approval of 
the members in a general meeting.
 The maximum number of Options that may be 
granted, in one or more tranches, pursuant to this 
Scheme shall not exceed 2,50,000 (Two Lakh Fifty 
Thousand) Options which shall be convertible into 
equal number of Shares not exceeding 2,50,000 
(Two Lakh Fifty Thousand) Equity Shares having 
face value of H 10/- each.
j) The Maximum quantum of benefits to be 
provided per Employee under the scheme:
 The maximum quantum of benefits that will be 
provided to every eligible employee under the 
Scheme will be the difference between the market 
value of Company’s share on the Recognized Stock 
Exchanges as on the Date of Exercise of Options 
and the Exercise Price paid by the Employee.
k) Whether the Scheme(s) is to be implemented 
and administered directly by the Company or 
through a Trust:
 The Scheme shall be implemented through 
direct route for extending the benefits to the 
eligible Employees by the way of fresh allotment 
from the Company.
 The Scheme shall be administered by the 
Nomination and Remuneration Committee 
of the Company.
l) Whether the Scheme involves new issue 
of shares by the company or secondary 
acquisition by the Trust or both:
 The Scheme involves new issue of Equity Shares 
by the Company.

m) The amount of loan to be provided for 
implementation of the Scheme by the 
Company to the Trust, its tenure, utilization, 
repayment terms, etc.:
 Not applicable, since the Scheme is proposed to be

n) The Maximum percentage of secondary

o) Disclosure and accounting policies:
 The Company shall comply with the disclosures 
requirements and the accounting policies 
prescribed under Regulation 15 of the SEBI (SBEB 
& SE) Regulations or as may be prescribed by 
regulatory authorities from time to time. 
p) The method which the Company shall use to 
value its Options:
 The Company shall comply with the requirements 
of IND – AS 102 and shall use Fair value method 
and the fair value of Options would be calculated 
as per the prescribed method under the 
applicable regulations.
q) Statement with regard to Disclosure in Board’s 
Report:
 As the company is adopting fair value method, 
presently there is no requirement for disclosure in 
board’s report. However, if in future, the Company opts for expensing of share based employee 
benefits using the intrinsic value, then the 
difference between the employee compensation 
cost so computed and the employee compensation 
cost that shall have been recognized if it had used 
the fair value, shall be disclosed in the Board’s 
Report and the impact of this difference on profits 
and on earnings per share (“EPS”) of the company 
shall also be disclosed in the Board’s Report.
r) Period of lock-in:
 The Shares allotted to the Grantees pursuant to 
Exercise of Options shall be subject to no lock-in 
period from the date of allotment. The Grantee is 
free to sell the shares. 
s) Terms & conditions for buyback, if any, of 
specified securities:
 The Committee has the powers to determine the 
procedure for buy-back of Options granted under 
the Scheme, if to be undertaken at any time by the 
Company, and the applicable terms and conditions, 
in accordance with the applicable law.

Expanding
Healthcare is undergoing a significant 
transformation, driven by increasing disease 
complexity and the growing demand for 
accessible specialised care. Against this 
evolving landscape, our strategic focus is to 
extend advanced clinical capabilities beyond 
conventional healthcare hubs. During the 
year, we expanded our presence across high-
potential markets, accelerated the ramp-
up of new facilities and strengthened our 
capabilities across tertiary and quaternary 
care specialities. Our continued investments 
in advanced medical technology, specialised 
infrastructure and clinical talent are enabling 
us to deliver increasingly complex procedures 
and sophisticated clinical interventions across 
our network.
Our expansion is underpinned by the 
development of an integrated and 
interconnected healthcare ecosystem. As 
our network grows, our centres of excellence 
are designed to complement one another 
through clinical collaboration, knowledge 
sharing and optimal resource utilisation, 
thereby enhancing access to specialised 
healthcare while maintaining consistency in 
clinical standards and outcomes.Our newer facilities in New Delhi, Faridabad 
and Agra are already demonstrating the 
potential of this model, while our forthcoming 
Gurugram facility and planned expansions will 
further extend our reach. Our international 
collaborations and medical value travel initiatives 
are also opening the network to patients beyond 
our immediate geography. 
For us, going beyond boundaries is therefore 
not about geographic expansion alone. It is 
about taking the depth of specialised medicine, 
advanced technology and clinical excellence to 
more patients, more markets and, ultimately, to 
places where such care has been less accessible. 
This is the direction in which Yatharth Hospitals 
is building its next phase of growth: expanding 
its footprint while raising the breadth, depth 
and quality of care across the network.Specialised
Healthcare
Beyond BoundariesContents

About Us
Yatharth is one of North India’s leading private healthcare 
networks, operating a portfolio of large multi-speciality hospitals 
focused on tertiary, quaternary and critical care services. Since our 
establishment in 2008, we have built a strong presence across key 
micro-markets in the National Capital Region while extending our 
footprint to the Jhansi-Orchha region.
Our hospitals are designed to serve diverse healthcare needs through substantial bed capacity and 
comprehensive clinical expertise across multiple specialties. By strategically expanding in densely populated 
catchment areas, we have strengthened access to advanced healthcare and established ourselves as a trusted 
provider within the NCR healthcare ecosystem.
Our Vision
Our Quality PolicyOur MissionTo evolve as the most preferred 
destination for quality healthcare 
that provides a comprehensive 
range of services and is trusted for 
personalized care with compassion.
To constantly upgrade ourselves to 
keep pace with what is new in the field 
to deliver healthcare, recruit highly 
qualified doctors and further improve 
clinical outcomes, patient safety and 
patient satisfaction. Committed to deliver quality and 
personalised care to improve 
the well-being of patients and 
communities we serve.
What We do
We deliver integrated healthcare services across the entire care continuum, combining 24/7 emergency 
and trauma care, primary healthcare, and advanced tertiary interventions. Our hospitals are equipped to 
manage both routine and high-acuity cases, ensuring timely access to specialised treatment through a 
coordinated, patient-centric approach.
Our network is supported by round-the-clock emergency response systems, modular ICUs, advanced 
anaesthesia and peri-operative care, and comprehensive diagnostic capabilities. From emergency triage 
and imaging to complex procedures and post-treatment support, we provide a seamless, clinically 
integrated care pathway at every stage of treatment.

Chairman’s 
Message
Dear 
Shareholders,FY 2025–26 has been a 
year of strengthening our 
presence and extending the 
boundaries of specialised 
healthcare. From our 
established centres in the 
Delhi-NCR region to our 
growing presence across 
cities of Haryana and other 
strategic locations, we 
have continued to build 
a healthcare network 
designed to bring high-
quality, technology-enabled 
and comprehensive 
medical care closer to the 
communities we serve. The future of healthcare is 
shaped by the investments 
we make today.
It gives me immense pleasure 
to present the Annual Report 
for FY 2025–26 at a defining 
stage in Yatharth’s journey—one 
marked by purposeful expansion, 
deeper clinical capabilities and 
an unwavering commitment to 
making specialised healthcare more 
accessible. As healthcare needs 
become increasingly complex and expectations continue to evolve, we 
believe that meaningful progress 
requires more than expanding 
capacity; it requires investing in 
advanced infrastructure, cutting-
edge technology, specialised 
medical expertise and, above all, a 
culture centred on patient care.
FY 2025–26 has been a year of 
strengthening our presence and 
extending the boundaries of 
specialised healthcare. From our 
established centres in the Delhi-
NCR region to our growing presence 
across cities of Haryana and 
other strategic locations, we have 
continued to build a healthcare 
network designed to bring high-
quality, technology-enabled and 
comprehensive medical care 
closer to the communities we 
serve. Our expanding footprint 
now encompasses key locations 
including Greater Noida, Noida, 
Noida Extension, Faridabad, Greater 
Faridabad, New Delhi, Jhansi-
Orchha, Agra and Gurugram, with 
the Group’s total capacity having 
crossed 2,800 beds.
This expansion reflects more than 
geographical growth. It represents 
our endeavour to take specialised 
healthcare beyond traditional 
boundaries—connecting patients 
with advanced clinical expertise, 
modern medical infrastructure 
and comprehensive treatment 
capabilities irrespective of where 
they live. Each new facility 
strengthens our ability to address a 
wider spectrum of healthcare needs 
while creating a consistent standard 
of quality, safety and patient 
experience across our network.
Our growth has been supported by 
sustained investments in medical 
infrastructure and technology, 
alongside the development of a 
strong team of doctors, nurses, 
paramedical professionals and 
healthcare specialists. Our 
accreditation journey, including JCI 
accreditation for our Greater Noida 
West hospital, reflects our continued 
focus on globally benchmarked quality, patient safety and clinical 
processes.
As we look ahead, our vision 
remains clear: to expand specialised 
healthcare beyond boundaries 
while ensuring that scale never 
comes at the expense of quality, 
compassion or trust. We will 
continue to invest thoughtfully in 
new markets, advanced clinical 
capabilities, technology and people, 
while strengthening the hospitals 
and communities we already serve.
Guided by our legacy of trust and 
driven by innovation, Yatharth is 
building an integrated healthcare 
ecosystem where advanced 
infrastructure, clinical excellence 
and compassionate care come 
together. We remain committed 
to creating greater access to 
specialised healthcare, delivering 
superior patient outcomes and 
building an institution that is 
resilient, responsible and future-
ready.
Expanding the Frontiers of 
Care
The Indian healthcare sector 
continues to offer significant 
opportunities for providers 
capable of delivering specialised, 
high-quality care at scale. Rising 
healthcare awareness and a 
growing preference for accredited 
multi-speciality hospitals are 
driving demand for integrated care, 
advanced clinical expertise and 
comprehensive treatment under 
one roof.
During the year, we strengthened 
our footprint through strategic 
expansion into New Delhi, Faridabad 
and Gurugram, further extending 
access to specialised healthcare 
across key markets. A significant 
milestone was our appointment 
as the exclusive hospital partner 
to Noida International Airport, 
strengthening our ability to serve 
international travellers, expatriates 
and non-resident Indians seeking 
trusted and accredited healthcare.International medical value travel 
is emerging as a measured second 
engine of growth. We deepened 
our relationships in Nigeria, 
conducted health camps and 
physician engagements across 
Uzbekistan and Tajikistan, and 
initiated outreach through the 
African Students Union. Building on 
these efforts, we plan to establish 
information centres in Uzbekistan, 
Tajikistan, Mauritius, Tanzania and 
Kenya, while engaging with health 
authorities in Iraq and Congo.
Our objective is to build predictable 
international referral channels 
and strengthen pre-arrival care 
planning. This approach will enable 
us to grow international patient 
volumes, improve ARPOB and 
occupancy, and create incremental 
growth while ensuring that our 
expanding international presence 
complements—and does not 
compromise—access to quality 
healthcare for domestic patients.
Strong Fundamentals. 
Stronger Performance.
Our strategic initiatives and 
disciplined execution translated 
into another year of strong financial 
performance. Consolidated revenue 
increased by 36% to ₹12,072 million, 
while EBITDA rose to ₹2,921 million, 
representing an EBITDA margin of 
24.2%. Profit After Tax (PAT) stood 
at ₹1,703 million, supported by 
operating cash flow, which reached 
₹2,866 million and an impressive 
cash conversion of 98%. We 
concluded the year with a net cash 
position of ₹1,160 million, providing a 
strong financial platform to support 
our long-term growth ambitions. 
These results reflect higher 
utilisation across our network, the 
progressive maturity of our newer 
hospitals and our prudent approach 
to capital allocation.
Delivering Clinical Excellence 
at a Greater Scale
Our operational performance 
further reinforced the strength of 
STATUTORY REPORTS FINANCIAL STATEMENTS CORPORATE OVERVIEW09
 08YATHARTH SUPER SPECIALITY HOSPITAL
ANNUAL REPORT 2025-26
our integrated healthcare model. 
Group occupancy improved to 68% 
from 61%, with occupancy reaching 
71% in the fourth quarter as our 
newer hospitals gained momentum 
alongside our more established 
facilities. At the same time, we 
achieved a meaningful shift in 
our specialty mix. Neurosciences 
increased its contribution to 
13% of group revenue from 11%, 
complemented by sustained growth 
across cardiology, orthopaedics, 
nephrology and general surgery. 
This increasing focus on higher-
acuity specialties enhanced 
revenue per occupied bed, resulting 
in ARPOB rising to ₹33,124.
Our hospitals at Noida Extension 
and Greater Noida recorded 
their highest-ever ARPOB of 
₹47,800 and ₹40,300 respectively, 
demonstrating the value of a 
mature referral network supported 
by specialised clinical capabilities. 
Importantly, these gains were 
achieved without compromising 
operational efficiency. Average 
Length of Stay remained stable at 
4.16 days even as inpatient volumes 
increased to approximately 88,000 
and outpatient footfalls reached 
around 427,000 during the year.
Underlying these achievements 
is our unwavering commitment 
to clinical excellence and quality. 
The JCI accreditation of our Noida 
Extension hospital, together 
with NABH accreditations across 
our network, reflects the robust 
clinical governance, patient safety 
standards and quality systems that 
define our institution.
People, Purpose and 
Governance
While infrastructure and technology 
enable our growth, our people bring 
our purpose to life. Our doctors, 
nurses, clinicians and support teams 
combine expertise, compassion and 
commitment to deliver consistent, 
patient-centric care across our 
expanding network. Supported 
by strong clinical governance and a culture of accountability, they 
remain the cornerstone of our 
clinical excellence.
As we expand specialised 
healthcare beyond boundaries, 
we continue to invest in building a 
strong talent pipeline. During the 
year, we strengthened partnerships 
with leading medical institutions 
and clinicians to develop specialists, 
nurses and allied healthcare 
professionals. Our DNB Residency 
Programme, spanning 13 
specialties, further strengthened 
our commitment to nurturing the 
next generation of medical talent.
Our growth is 
powered by people—
and our commitment 
to excellence begins 
with them.  
I extend my sincere gratitude to 
our Board of Directors for their 
guidance; our doctors, nurses and 
employees for their exceptional 
commitment; our partners for their 
enduring collaboration; our patients 
for placing their trust in us; and our 
shareholders for their continued confidence. Your support inspires 
us to raise the benchmark for 
healthcare delivery and strengthens 
our resolve to build an institution 
that will serve generations to come.
Warm Regards
Dr. Ajay Kumar Tyagi
Chairman and Whole-time Director

Noida Extension
We operate Yatharth Super Speciality Hospital, 
Noida Extension (Greater Noida West), Uttar 
Pradesh, as a tertiary care facility and a key hub for 
our super-specialty services. The hospital houses 
multiple Centres of Excellence across oncology, 
organ transplantation, neurosciences, cardiology, 
and robotic surgery. In March 2024, we further 
strengthened our advanced care capabilities 
with the inauguration of a dedicated radiation 
oncology unit.
The hospital is the first and only healthcare 
institution in Uttar Pradesh to receive the 
prestigious Joint Commission International (JCI) 
accreditation, reaffirming our commitment to 
delivering world-class, patient-centric care.

Seeding the Next Cluster, Gurugram
As part of our cluster-based expansion strategy, we have strengthened our presence in the National Capital 
Region through the acquisition of an under-construction, ultra-modern super-speciality hospital in Sector 
40, Gurugram. Strategically located in Central Gurugram, the facility is positioned within a high-income 
catchment area comprising established residential communities, premium commercial districts and key 
corporate corridors.
Designed as a next-generation healthcare destination, the hospital will address the evolving healthcare 
needs of domestic and international patients. Expected to commence operations in April 2027, the facility will 
further reinforce our presence across the NCR healthcare ecosystem.

Quality Management Advancing Clinical Excellence 
and Technology
Delivering safe, high-quality and patient-centric care remains 
central to our operating philosophy. We have established a robust 
clinical governance framework across our network, supported by 
dedicated hospital committees overseeing infection prevention and 
control, antimicrobial stewardship, patient outcomes and trends in 
unplanned readmissions.Our clinical model is built around Centres of Excellence that unite 
multidisciplinary expertise, specialised infrastructure and evidence-
based treatment protocols to deliver integrated, outcome-led care. 
Insights from these reviews are converted into defined corrective actions, with implementation tracked at the 
unit level to ensure timely closure and continuous improvement under the supervision of clinical and operational 
leaders.By combining advanced medical technologies with deep clinical expertise, these centres enable us to manage 
complex healthcare needs while ensuring consistent quality, clinical excellence and a patient-centric experience 
across our network. As the cornerstone of our tertiary care platform, they drive sustained patient volumes  across 
Operating Theatres (OT), Intensive Care Unit (ICU) and day-care facilities. 
Embedding Quality by 
Design
Accreditations and 
CertificationsFor new hospitals, quality 
standards are integrated from the 
planning stage, with processes 
and documentation designed to 
align with NABH requirements. 
In parallel, laboratory protocols 
are structured in accordance 
with NABL standards to ensure 
reliable and accurate diagnostics 
across our network.
Our commitment to clinical 
excellence is reflected in our 
accreditation journey. Yatharth 
Hospital, Noida Extension, is 
the first and only hospital in 
Uttar Pradesh to receive the 
prestigious Joint Commission 
International (JCI) accreditation. 
Additionally, all our hospitals, 
[except Faridabad Sec 20 and 
New Delhi Hospitals are under 
progress (inspection done)], have 
secured NABH accreditation.
Our laboratories are NABL 
certified across all locations, 
excluding Jhansi and Faridabad.

interventional procedures, along 
with high-precision imaging and 
CT-like visualisation.As we scale our network, we continue to invest in medical education and 
talent development through our Diplomate of National Board (DNB) 
programme. With 19 specialties and 74 resident doctors enrolled across 
Noida, Noida Extension, and Greater Noida, the programme strengthens 
our clinical talent pipeline while reinforcing our commitment to excellence 
in healthcare delivery.
Yatharth Hospital, Agra, acheved a 
significant milestone by successfully 
performing the city’s first Da Vinci 
robotic surgeries. Four advanced 
procedures were conducted in a 
single day across urology, oncology, 
cardiology, gastroenterology and 
gynaecology, underscoring our 
growing expertise in robotic-
assisted interventions and advanced 
surgical care.In a landmark achievement, 
specialists at Yatharth Hospital, 
Sector 88, Faridabad, performed 
Asia’s first scarless endoscopic 
tumour removal surgery. The 
innovative procedure enabled 
the removal of a complex tumour 
through the mouth, eliminating 
the need for major incisions while 
significantly reducing recovery time 
and post-operative complications.
Our multidisciplinary team at 
Yatharth Hospital, Sector 88, 
Faridabad, successfully treated 
an 11-year-old girl suffering from 
severe respiratory distress caused 
by a rare bronchial condition. 
Using advanced bronchoscopy 
techniques, doctors removed the 
obstruction and restored normal 
breathing, delivering a life-changing 
outcome.Demonstrating our expertise in 
minimally invasive procedures, 
Yatharth Hospital, Faridabad, 
successfully corrected a congenital 
heart defect in a five-year-old 
child through minimally invasive 
cardiac surgery. The approach 
avoided major incisions, enabling 
faster recovery and improved post-
operative outcomes.
We further strengthened our 
oncology capabilities through the 
successful deployment of Hybrid 
Argon Plasma Coagulation (Hybrid 
APC) technology for treating 
food pipe cancer. The advanced 
technique enabled precise removal 
of cancerous tissue while preserving 
healthy structures, reflecting our 
commitment to precision-led

Board of Directors
Ms. Promila BhardwajMr. Ramesh Krishnan
Independent DirectorIndependent Director
Ms. Promila Bhardwaj has been serving as an Independent Director 
on the Board of Yatharth Hospital & Trauma Care Services Limited 
since October 10, 2022. She brings with her a distinguished career in 
public service, with over 35 years of experience in public administration. 
 
She holds a Bachelor of Arts and a Master of Arts in English from Punjab 
University, along with a Master of Philosophy in Social Sciences from the  
same institution. Additionally, she holds a Master’s Diploma in Public 
Administration from the Indian Institute of Public Administration (IIPA), 
Certificate in Global Leadership and Governance from Wharton School 
of Business, University of Pennsylvania and Indian Institute of  
Management (IIM), Bangalore.
Ms. Bhardwaj joined the Indian Revenue Services (“IRS”) in 1979 and held 
several senior roles throughout her tenure like Deputy Secretary Under 
Ministry of Water Resources, Director of Industrial Policy and Promotion, 
Personal Secretary to Minister of State for Women and Child Development, 
Director General (HRD) (International Taxation & Transfer Pricing) &  
(Systems) and Chief Commissioner of Income Tax. She retired as the  
Director General of Income Tax. Her vast expertise in governance,  
public policy, systems implementation, and administrative leadership  
brings immense value to the Company’s Board, particularly in matters 
related to compliance, strategic oversight, and institutional integrity.Mr Ramesh Krishnan is a seasoned healthcare professional with over  
three decades of experience across hospital management, private equity  
and strategic consulting. He has extensive expertise in healthcare strategy, 
hospital operations, business expansion, acquisitions and post-merger  
integrations across multiple geographies.
Prior to this, he served as an Operating Partner at Everstone Capital from 
September 2018 to June 2023, where he led the acquisition of the Sahyadri 
Hospitals network by Everstone Capital. From October 2013 to August 2018, 
Mr. Krishnan served as CEO – South Asia, Parkway Pantai (IHH Healthcare), 
where he was responsible for overseeing multiple hospital acquisitions 
and their integration. Earlier, from November 2010 to October 2013, he 
served as Vice President, Fortis Healthcare, Singapore, where he led post-
merger integrations and bolt-on acquisitions across multiple geographies. 
 
With his extensive experience in healthcare management, strategic 
planning, acquisitions and integration, Mr. Krishnan brings valuable industry 
knowledge and strategic perspective to the Board.
Dr. Ajay Kumar Tyagi
Chairman & Whole-time Director
Dr. Ajay Kumar Tyagi has been associated with the medical profession 
for over four decades. A graduate from LLRM Medical College, Meerut, he 
specialises in orthopaedics and has played a pivotal role in shaping the 
growth and evolution of Yatharth Hospitals. Before entering the private 
healthcare sector, he served with the Directorate of Health & Family Welfare, 
Government of Uttar Pradesh. Under his leadership and strategic guidance, 
the Group has expanded its footprint across the NCR and beyond, while 
maintaining a strong focus on clinical excellence and patient-centric care.
Mr. Yatharth Tyagi
Whole-time Director
Mr. Yatharth Tyagi joined the Company in 2019 and was appointed to the 
Board in 2021. He holds a Bachelor’s degree in Business and Management 
and a Master’s degree in International Health Management from Imperial 
College London. As a member of the next generation of leadership, he plays 
a key role in driving strategic initiatives, business development and the 
expansion of the Company’s healthcare ecosystem, while supporting the 
adoption of modern technologies and innovative care models.
Dr. Kapil Kumar
Managing Director
Dr. Kapil Kumar has been associated with Yatharth Hospitals since its 
inception and has been instrumental in driving its operational and clinical 
growth. An alumnus of SN Medical College, Agra, and a renowned orthopaedic 
surgeon, he brings extensive experience in hospital administration and 
healthcare management. He has previously served in various prestigious 
institutions in Delhi and currently oversees the day-to-day operations of the 
Group’s hospitals. He is also actively involved in several social and healthcare 
initiatives.

Dr. Sanjeev Upadhyaya
Independent Director
Dr. Sanjeev Upadhyaya has been an Independent Director of 
the Company since February 21, 2022. He is a public health and 
development professional with over two decades of experience 
in public health and healthcare systems across India and Africa. 
 
Dr. Upadhyaya is a medical doctor with a degree from LLRM Medical 
College, Meerut, and specialises in Community Medicine from Mahatma 
Gandhi Institute of Medical Sciences (MGIMS), Sevagram, Wardha. He 
has also undergone training in Health Systems Strengthening from 
the Nossal Institute of Global Health, University of Melbourne, Australia. 
 
He has extensive experience in maternal, newborn and child health and 
infectious diseases and has worked with UNICEF for over a decade as a 
Health Specialist in India and Africa. He has also served as a Senior Advisor 
with the United States Agency for International Development (USAID), 
New Delhi, and has undertaken consulting assignments with various 
national and international organisations, including WHO, CARE, IntraHealth 
International, Handicap International, Futures Group International and the 
Ministry of Health & Family Welfare, Government of India.

of experience spanning banking, administration and public service. He 
holds degrees in science, animal husbandry and public administration and 
has served in senior leadership positions, including with Bank of Baroda. 
His broad experience across financial institutions and public administration 
provides valuable insights into governance, risk management and 
organisational development.

Mr. Ritesh Mishra is a Qualified Company Secretary and an Associate 
Member of the Institute of Company Secretaries of India and holds a 
Bachelor of Laws (LL.B.) degree. He has also completed his Master of 
Business Administration (MBA) from Sikkim Manipal University and holds a 
Bachelor’s degree in Commerce (B.Com.) from Awadh University, Faizabad. 
 
He is a seasoned professional with rich experience of over 10 years in 
corporate law, secretarial matters and regulatory compliances. He 
possesses sound knowledge of corporate governance, securities laws, 
regulatory frameworks and compliances applicable to listed entities. 
He is actively involved in supporting the Board and its Committees in 
the effective discharge of their responsibilities and in ensuring timely 
and appropriate compliance with applicable laws and regulations. 
 
He has been instrumental in strengthening the Company’s corporate 
governance framework, coordinating with the Board and its Committees, 
facilitating statutory and regulatory compliances and ensuring effective 
stakeholder communication. He also closely monitors developments 
in the regulatory and economic environment and evaluates their 
implications for the healthcare business and its stakeholders.

2. STATE OF COMPANY AFFAIRS
 The Company has continued to make significant 
progress in its growth journey, further 
strengthening its position as a trusted healthcare 
provider and laying a robust foundation for long-
term, sustainable growth. During the year, the 
Company expanded its healthcare footprint to 
a network of nine hospitals, comprising seven 
strategically located facilities across the Delhi-NCR 
region, one hospital in Agra, Uttar Pradesh, and 
one hospital in Jhansi-Orchha, Madhya Pradesh. 
 Over the past fiscal year, the Company has 
demonstrated a consistent commitment to 
excellence in healthcare delivery, maintaining 
a strong focus on operational efficiency, service 
quality, and patient care. This sustained momentum 
reflects not only the achievements of previous years 
but also a clear vision for long-term leadership in the 
healthcare sector. By continuously strengthening 
its presence and enhancing service standards, the 
Company is well-positioned to capitalize on future 
opportunities and contribute meaningfully to the 
evolving healthcare landscape in India. The year witnessed significant growth in the 
Company's financial performance. On a standalone 
basis, Revenue from Operations increased to 
H5,918.84 million during FY 2025-26 from H4,564.84 
million in the previous year, registering a growth of 
29.66%. Profit after Tax increased to H954.10 million 
from H876.92 million in the previous year, reflecting 
a growth of 8.80%.
 On a consolidated basis, Revenue from Operations 
increased to H12,071.72 million from H8,856.21 
million in FY 2024-25, representing a robust growth 
of 36.31%. Consolidated Profit after Tax stood at 
H1,703.05 million as compared to H1,305.50 million in 
the previous year, registering a growth of 30.45%. 
Total Comprehensive Income on a consolidated 
basis increased to H1,708.78 million from H1,308.43 
million in the previous year.
 The Company's strong financial performance was 
supported by improved operational efficiencies, 
increased patient volumes, and the continued 
contribution from its expanding hospital network. 
Earnings per Equity Share (EPS) on a standalone 
basis increased to H9.90 from H9.89, while 
STATUTORY REPORTS FINANCIAL STATEMENTS CORPORATE OVERVIEW37
consolidated EPS increased to H18.20 from H14.72 
during the year under review.
 This strong financial performance highlights the 
Company’s commitment to operational excellence, 
strategic expansion, and the consistent delivery of 
high-quality healthcare services. By sustaining the 
momentum built in previous years, the Company 
remains committed to delivering sustainable growth, 
creating value for stakeholders, and contributing 
meaningfully to India's evolving healthcare landscape.
3. TRANSFER TO RESERVES
 As part of its prudent financial management, the 
Company has transferred J 954.10 million of its 
profit to general reserves, thereby strengthening 
its financial stability and capacity for future 
investments. No profit was allocated to any 
specified reserves during the year.
4. DIVIDEND
 Pursuant to regulation 43A(1) of the Securities and 
Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations and 
Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 2015 ('SEBI 
Listing Regulations'), the Board had formulated 
a Dividend Distribution Policy (‘the Policy’). The 
Policy is available on the Company’s website at 
https://www.yatharthhospitals.com/investors/
corporate-policies .
 The Board is of the view that conserving internal 
resources and reinvesting earnings into the Company's 
growth initiatives will enhance its operational 
capacity, support sustainable business development, 
and generate long-term value for shareholders. 
Accordingly, final dividend has been recommended 
for the financial year ended March 31, 2026.
 The Company is, however, proposed to declare 
an Interim Dividend during the financial year 
2026-27,  with a view to appropriately balance the 
interests of its shareholders with the Company’s 
requirements for continued growth, expansion 
and strengthening of its healthcare infrastructure.
5. TRANSFER OF UNCLAIMED DIVIDEND 
TO INVESTOR EDUCATION AND

 The provisions of Section 124 and 125(2) of the

(Accounting, Audit, Transfer and Refund) Rules,

15. PARTICULARS OF CONSERVATION OF 
ENERGY, TECHNOLOGY ABSORPTION 
AND FOREIGN EXCHANGE EARNINGS 
AND OUTGO
 The provisions of Section 134(m) of the Companies 
Act, 2013 read with Rule 8(3) of the Companies 
{The Companies (Accounts)} Rules, 2014 are 
given as below:
 A. Conservation of Energy
  i. Steps taken or impact on conservation 
of energy
   In accordance with the provisions of 
Section 134(3)(m) of the Companies Act, 
2013, the Company continues to prioritize 
energy conservation and sustainable 
operational practices. During the year 
under review, the Company undertook 
various initiatives aimed at improving 
energy efficiency, optimizing resource 
utilization and reducing overall energy 
consumption. These measures include 
the use of energy-efficient LED lighting, adoption of low power consumption 
equipment and systems, optimization 
of air-conditioning solutions, enabling 
energy-saving modes across IT 
infrastructure and increased deployment 
of energy-efficient appliances.
   A significant step towards sustainable 
energy management has been the 
Company's continued association 
with Fourth Partner Solar Power 
Private Limited for sourcing renewable 
energy through captive solar power 
arrangements. The initiative has 
progressed substantially and has enabled 
the Company to increasingly utilize solar 
energy for its operations.
  2. The steps taken by the Company for 
utilizing alternate sources of energy
   The Company has continued to promote 
the use of renewable energy through 
captive solar power arrangements in 
association with Fourth Partner Solar 
Power Private Limited. During the year 
under review, captive solar power usage 
commenced at the Company's three 
Hospitals situated at Noida, Greater 
Noida and Greater Noida West, thereby 
increasing the utilization of renewable 
energy for its operations.
   The initiative has contributed to reducing 
dependence on conventional electricity 
sources and supports the Company's 
objectives of energy efficiency, 
cost optimization, environmental 
sustainability and responsible 
business practices.
 B. Technology Absorption
  1. Efforts made towards technology 
absorption
   During the year under review, the 
Company continued to enhance its 
healthcare capabilities through the 
adoption and integration of advanced 
medical technologies aimed at improving 
diagnostic accuracy, treatment outcomes, 
and patient care.
   Key technological advancements include 
the installation of advanced Oncology 
Equipment and PET-CT (Positron 
Emission Tomography–Computed 
Tomography) technology, strengthening 
cancer diagnosis, staging, and treatment 
planning capabilities; adoption of 
Robotic-Assisted Knee Replacement 
STATUTORY REPORTS FINANCIAL STATEMENTS CORPORATE OVERVIEW41
Surgery technology, enabling greater 
surgical precision, improved implant 
alignment, and enhanced patient 
outcomes; and deployment of the Da 
Vinci Robotic Surgical System, which 
has further strengthened minimally 
invasive surgical capabilities by providing 
surgeons with enhanced precision, 
flexibility, and control.
   The Company continues to integrate 
innovative medical technologies and 
advanced healthcare solutions across its 
operations, reaffirming its commitment 
to delivering quality, technology-enabled, 
and patient-centric healthcare services.
  2. Benefits derived from such technology 
absorption
   The adoption and integration of these 
advanced technologies have enhanced 
the Company’s diagnostic and therapeutic 
capabilities, improved surgical precision, 
supported better treatment planning and 
outcomes, and strengthened the delivery 
of minimally invasive and patient-centric 
healthcare services.
 C. Foreign Exchange Earnings and Outgo

17. BUSINESS RESPONSIBILITY AND 
SUSTAINABILITY REPORT
 Business Responsibility and Sustainability Report 
for FY 2025-26, as stipulated under Regulation 34(2)
(f) SEBI Listing Regulations, is presented under a 
separate section forming part of this Annual Report.18. STATEMENT BY DIRECTOR RESPONSIBLE 
FOR THE BUSINESS RESPONSIBILITY 
REPORT, HIGHLIGHTING ESG RELATED 
CHALLENGES, TARGETS AND 
ACHIEVEMENTS
 A structured and strategic approach to 
sustainability remains at the heart of our initiatives. 
The Company has advanced efforts in reducing 
its environmental footprint by continuing to 
optimise energy consumption and water use, 
while adhering to stringent protocols in the 
management and disposal of bio-medical waste. 
These efforts align with the broader vision of 
mitigating environmental impact while ensuring 
the health and safety of patients, employees, and 
the communities we serve.
 a. Environmental, Social, and Governance 
(ESG) Initiatives
  Your Company continues its commitment 
as a responsible healthcare institution by 
advancing its Environmental, Social, and 
Governance (ESG) agenda. Building upon 
the foundations laid in previous years, 
ESG principles have become increasingly 
integrated into the Company’s operational 
practices, strategic decision-making, and 
stakeholder engagement efforts.
 b. Environmental Stewardship
  The Company maintains a structured and 
strategic approach to sustainability, with 
ongoing initiatives focused on reducing its 
environmental footprint. Key areas of focus 
include the optimization of energy and water 
usage, and the adoption of stringent standards 
for bio-medical waste management. These 
initiatives are aligned with our broader mission 
to minimize environmental impact while 
prioritizing the health and safety of patients, 
staff, and the wider community.
  In full compliance with the Bio-Medical 
Waste Management Rules, 2016, prescribed 
by the Government of India, the Company 
has implemented a robust framework for the 
monitoring, segregation, treatment, and safe 
disposal of bio-medical waste. This ensures 
not only legal compliance but also contributes 
meaningfully to environmental sustainability.
 c. Social Responsibility
  The Company remains deeply committed 
to fostering a people-centric culture. Our 
employee welfare initiatives promote 
inclusivity, occupational health and safety, 
professional development, and work-
YATHARTH SUPER SPECIALITY HOSPITAL
ANNUAL REPORT 2025-2642
life balance. The Company maintains a  
zero-tolerance policy against discrimination 
and harassment and is dedicated to creating 
a workplace grounded in fairness, respect, and 
equal opportunity.
 d. Ethical Governance
  Strong corporate governance continues to 
form the backbone of the Company’s value 
system. All Directors, senior management, 
and employees operate under a strict code of 
ethics, emphasizing integrity, accountability, 
and compliance. Whistleblower mechanisms 
are in place to enable employees to 
confidentially report unethical conduct, policy 
violations, or compliance concerns without 
fear of retaliation.
 As the Company advances in its ESG journey, 
it remains focused on delivering high-quality 
healthcare while driving sustainable, inclusive 
growth. The integration of ESG principles into 
the Company’s strategic roadmap underscores 
its commitment to long-term value creation 
for all stakeholders and to positioning itself as 
a responsible and forward-looking leader in the 
healthcare sector.

for determining remuneration in accordance 
with the applicable statutory requirements and 
prevailing industry practices, while ensuring that 
the remuneration structure is aligned with the 
Company’s objectives and long-term interests.
 The Nomination and Remuneration Committee

oversees its implementation. The salient features 
of the Nomination and Remuneration Policy are 
disclosed in the Corporate Governance Report, 
forming part of the Annual Report.

29. INTERNAL FINANCIAL CONTROLS AND 
SYSTEMS 
 The Company has in place an adequate internal 
financial control framework, commensurate with 
the size, scale and complexity of its operations, 
designed to ensure orderly and efficient conduct 
of business, safeguarding of assets, prevention 
and detection of frauds and errors, accuracy and 
completeness of accounting records, and timely 
preparation of reliable financial information.
 The adequacy and operating effectiveness of the 
internal controls are periodically reviewed through 
the Internal Audit function, which undertakes risk-
based audits across various functions and locations. 
The Internal Audit function operates independently 
and reports its significant observations to the Audit 
Committee, which reviews the audit findings, 
corrective actions and overall effectiveness of the 
internal control framework.
 Based on the assessment carried out during the 
year under review, the Board is of the opinion 
that the Company has adequate internal financial 
controls with reference to the Financial Statements 
and that such controls were operating effectively 
as at March 31, 2026.30. MATERIAL CHANGES AND 
COMMITMENTS, IF ANY, AFFECTING THE 
FINANCIAL POSITION OF THE COMPANY 
OCCURRED BETWEEN THE END OF 
THE FINANCIAL YEAR TO WHICH THIS 
FINANCIAL STATEMENTS RELATE AND 
THE DATE OF THE REPORT
 During and subsequent to the year under 
review, the Company continued to strengthen its 
healthcare network through strategic acquisitions 
and expanding its healthcare network and 
increasing patient capacity. 
 The Company acquired 100% controlling stake in 
M/s Shanti Ved Institute of Medical Sciences Private 
Limited, Agra, Uttar Pradesh, a 150-bedded super-
speciality hospital with expansion potential up to 
250 beds, thereby enhancing the Group's footprint 
in Uttar Pradesh. Further, the Company, through its 
subsidiary M/s MGS Infotech Research & Solutions 
Private Limited, commenced operations of its 
hospital facility at Sector 20, Faridabad, Haryana 
on November 1, 2025, thereby strengthening 
the Group's healthcare presence and providing 
additional avenues for revenue generation
 Additionally, the Company, through its wholly 
owned subsidiary M/s AKS Medical & Research 
Centre Private Limited, completed the acquisition 
of a hospital asset at Sector 40, Gurugram, Haryana. 
The facility has a potential capacity of approximately 
250 beds and is expected to further strengthen the 
Company's presence in the Delhi-NCR region.
 With these additions, the Yatharth Group 
continues to expand its presence across key 
healthcare markets and remains focused on 
providing accessible, high-quality and technology-
driven healthcare services across a broader 
geographical base while creating sustainable value 
for all stakeholders.

 Income Tax Matter
 During the year under review, the Company 
received orders from the Income Tax Department 
releasing all provisionally attached properties and

under Section 132 of the Income-tax Act, 1961. The 
Company continues to extend full cooperation

STATUTORY REPORTS FINANCIAL STATEMENTS CORPORATE OVERVIEW45
of the Annual Report. Based on the information 
currently available, the Board does not foresee any 
material impact on the going concern status or 
future operations of the Company.
 Jhansi Orchha Hospital Matter 
 During the year under review, M/s Ramraja 
Multispeciality Hospital & Trauma Centre Private 
Limited, wholly owned subsidiary Company has 
received an order dated June 30, 2025, from the 
Commissionerate, whereby the earlier order dated 
July 4, 2022, relating to the Hospital land dispute 
was set aside. Consequently, the matter stands 
resolved in its favour, and no further proceedings 
were pending in this regard as on the date of 
this Report. The aforesaid order is procedural in 
nature and does not have any material impact 
on the going concern status, financial position, 
or future operations of the Company or its wholly 
owned subsidiary.
32. RISK MANAGEMENT 
 The Company has established a structured 
Risk Management Policy and Framework 
commensurate with the size, scale and complexity 
of its operations. The framework provides 
for systematic identification, assessment, 
prioritisation, mitigation and monitoring of risks 
that may affect the achievement of the Company’s 
strategic and business objectives.
 The Company’s risk management framework 
encompasses key areas of risk, including 
operational, financial, regulatory, technological, 
strategic, reputational and other business-related 
risks. Functional and business heads are responsible 
for identifying and monitoring risks within their 
respective areas and implementing appropriate 
mitigation measures. The identified risks and 
mitigation plans are reviewed periodically to ensure 
that emerging risks are appropriately addressed.
 The Risk Management Committee and the Board of 
Directors oversee the Company’s risk management 
framework and periodically review the key risks, 
mitigation measures and the effectiveness of the 
risk management processes. The framework is also 
supported by the Company’s internal control and 
internal audit mechanisms, thereby facilitating 
timely identification and mitigation of risks.
 The Company remains committed to continuously 
strengthening its risk management practices 
to enhance resilience, support informed 
decision-making and safeguard the interests of 
its stakeholders.
 The Risk Management Policy is available 
on the website of the Company and can be 
assessed under the tab of Corporate Policies at https://www.yatharthhospitals.com/investors/
corp orate-policies.

37. BOARD EVALUATION
 Pursuant to the provisions of the Section 134(3)(p) 
and Schedule IV the Act and Regulation 17(10) and 
25(4) of the SEBI Listing Regulations, the Board has 
carried out the annual performance evaluation of 
the Board as a whole, various Committees of the 
Board and of Individual Directors. The performance 
evaluation of Independent Directors was carried 
out by the entire Board of the Company.
 The evaluation was conducted under the guidance 
of the Nomination and Remuneration Committee, 
based on a structured evaluation framework and 
criteria approved by the Board. The assessment 
covered various aspects, including the composition 
and effectiveness of the Board and its Committees, 
quality of strategic oversight, governance practices, 
risk management and compliance oversight, effectiveness of decision-making processes, and 
the contribution and participation of individual 
Directors in the discharge of their duties and 
responsibilities.
 In a separate Meeting of Independent Directors, 
performance of Non-Independent Directors and 
Chairman of the Company was evaluated, taking 
into account the views of Executive Directors and 
Non-Executive Directors. The same was discussed 
in the Board Meeting that followed the Meeting of 
Independent Directors, at which the performance 
of the Board, its Committees and Individual 
Directors was also discussed.
 The outcomes of the evaluation were reviewed by 
the Board and the Nomination and Remuneration 
Committee, and the feedback received has been 
utilized to further strengthen the effectiveness 
of the Board and enhance overall corporate 
governance standards.
 A detailed description of the performance 
evaluation framework, methodology, and criteria 
is provided in the Corporate Governance Report, 
which forms an integral part of this Annual Report.

39. DETAILS OF DIFFERENCE BETWEEN 
AMOUNT OF THE VALUATION DONE AT 
THE TIME OF ONE TIME SETTLEMENT 
AND THE VALUATION DONE WHILE 
TAKING LOAN FROM THE BANKS OR 
FINANCIAL INSTITUTIONS ALONG WITH 
THE REASONS THEREOF 
 During the financial year under review, the Company 
has not entered into any One-Time Settlement (OTS) 
arrangement with any bank or financial institution 
for the settlement of its outstanding borrowings or 
financial obligations. The Company has continued 
to maintain its borrowings and discharge its 
financial commitments prudently in the ordinary 
course of business, and all repayments have been 
made in accordance with the respective terms and 
conditions of the financing arrangements. In view 
of the absence of any OTS transactions during the 
year, the disclosure requirements relating to the 
comparison of valuations carried out at the time of 
loan sanction and at the time of settlement are not 
applicable to the Company. Consequently, there is 
no difference in valuation to be reported, and no 
explanatory statement is required in this regard.

by its CSR Policy and are undertaken with the objective of promoting inclusive and sustainable development.

under the Act and the applicable rules.
